          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1.  The present special leave petition has been filed by
the petitioner for grant of bail in connection with FIR
No.41/2025 dated 21.02.2025 for the offences punishable
under Sections 70(2), 351(3), 61, 61(2)(A), 308(2), 308(6),
3(5), 199(c), 319(2) of the Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita, 2023
and Sections 6 of the The Protection of Children from
Sexual Offences Act, 2012 and Sections 3, 4, 5, 7 of the
Immoral Trafficking Act, Sections 3(2)(va), 3(2)(v) of the
Scheduled  Caste  and  Scheduled  Tribe  (Prevention  of
Atrocities) Act, 1989 and Sections 7, 12 of the Prevention
of Corruption Act, 1988 registered at Police Station City
Kaithal.
2.  The facts of the case reveals that the petitioner is
in  jail  for  offences  punishable  under  the  aforesaid
Sections. He is in jail for the last 1 years and 2 months.
The matter was taken up for the first time on 21.08.2026
and the request was made to list the matter on 01.09.2026.
The  matter  was  listed  on  01.09.2026  and  additional
documents  were  filed  by  the  learned  counsel  for  the
petitioner. The petitioner while filing I.A. No.259866/2026
has filed the statement of prosecution witnesses No.1
Annexure P-7 at page No.14 and extract of the aforesaid
statement reproduced as under:-
PETITION(S) FOR SPECIAL LEAVE TO APPEAL (CRL.)  NO(S).  16613/2026

“Stated that I am 17 years old and my date of
birth is 4.5.2008. My mother had left us in the
year 2013. We were staying with my father and my
grandmother. In the year 2014, my father also
died. Then my paternal grandmother left me at my
Bua's place. The attitude of my Bua was not good
towards me then I had left her house on 18th
September 2024 and went to Jawahar park. I was
sitting in that park when accused Balinder came
and by his talks, enticed me and took me with him
in R.N. hotel while saying that I can stay over
there. On this, I went with him. There he started
making physical relations with me. He also started
sending me to other men in other hotels. There
also, he used to get wrong act of making physical
relationships with me by those persons. Accused
Gagandeep was also working on the reception of
R.N. hotel. Accused Balinder by putting pressure
upon me got my documents of live-in relationship
executed with that Gagandeep, in this one Sonu

anyone, they will send me to Nepal. But I made
complaint in the month of January 2025 in Police-

Neetu who had not taken any action on my complaint
and had not given me any hearing as she was also
in connivance with accused Balinder.

2
Advocate General for the common respondents no.1 and 3 and Mr. Ajay

2. Leave granted.
3. These present appeals arise out of the impugned orders dated
17.11.2025 passed by the High Court of Punjab & Haryana at
Chandigarh in CRMM No.58768 of 2025 and CRMM No.62513 of 2025 by
which,  the respondent no.2 in both the matters had been granted
anticipatory bail.
4. The appellant made a complaint against the respondent no.2 in
both the matters with regard to certain orders, which the Shiv Raj
Singh  had  passed  purportedly  acting  as  the  Incharge  Land
Acquisition Officer especially with regard to giving two “No Dues
Certificates” dated 05.01.2022 and 21.02.2022 in the capacity of
the Land Acquisition Officer although, Shiv Raj Singh was neither
holding the said charge nor authorised to exercise the powers of
the Land Acquisition Officer on the relevant dates. 
5. Learned Senior Counsel for the appellant submitted that the
conduct of Shiv Raj Singh was not only illegal but beyond his
competence and jurisdiction as he was not the Incharge of the post
of the Land Acquisition Officer, Panchkula during the relevant
dates. It was further pointed out that the High Court  vide  its
impugned orders has noticed that the learned State Counsel has
taken a categorical stand that Shiv Raj Singh and Karamvir Singh
were cooperating with the investigation whereas, two days prior to
the  passing  of  the  impugned  orders,  the  State  had  filed  a
categorical affidavit that they had not been cooperating with the

--- Page 34 ---
3
investigation.
6. Learned Senior Additional Advocate General for the State of
Haryana drew the attention of the Court to the affidavit filed on
behalf of the State of Haryana in which, a categorical stand has
been taken that Shiv Raj Singh, who was substantively holding the
post of a Naib Tehsildar, was neither the regularly posted Land
Acquisition Officer nor the Officer entrusted with the charge of
Land Acquisition Officer, Panchkula during the relevant dates and
consequently,  the  issuance  of  “No  Dues  Certificates”   dated
05.01.2022 and 21.02.2022 by Shiv Raj Singh, was without authority
and beyond the scope of his official competence.
7. Learned counsel for the respondents no.2 in both the matters
submits that the respondents no.2 have been targeted for the reason
that the appellant himself is interested in the lands and the only
act of Shiv Raj Singh was accepting the money from the persons
whose lands had been acquired but later on, released by this Court,
whereas Karamvir Singh, being the Kanoongo had colluded in the
same. It was contended that due to this, the entire State machinery
had worked against the respondents no.2 in both the matters.
However, he submits that the fact is that Shiv Raj Singh was the
authorised person on the relevant dates and in the capacity of the
Land Acquisition Officer, Panchkula, he had issued the said “No
Dues Certificates” in collusion with Karamvir Singh, being the
Kanoongo. In support thereof, he drew the attention of the Court to
the information received by Shiv Raj Singh under the Right to
Information Act, 2005.

--- Page 35 ---
4
8. Having considered the rival contentions and going through the
materials on record, we find that the grant of anticipatory bail to
the respondents no.2 in both the matters, in the facts and
circumstances of the present case, was totally unjustified and
cannot be sustained. The act alleged against the respondents no.2
in both the matters has been well defined in the response filed by
the State of Haryana, which the Court can presume is a neutral
authority and would take a stand before the Court in an objective
manner. Both, before the High Court as well as before this Court,
we find that the State of Haryana has taken an unwavering stand
that the act of the respondents no.2 in both the cases and their
conduct has been beyond jurisdiction and in fact, illegal. At this
juncture, we note that in the so-called RTI query made by Shiv Raj
Singh to the Department, the date is shown as 07.08.2026 and the
reply which has been received by him indicates that the process of
giving reply on such query was initiated on 05.08.2026 itself. We
only indicate, without expressing our opinion as inferences are
obvious and startling. We hope that the State will take cognizance
of such conduct of its own officers in these matters, especially in
the facts and circumstances of the present cases.
9. For  reasons  aforesaid,  the  appeals  are  allowed.  The
anticipatory bail granted to the respondents no.2 in both the
matters by the High Court stand cancelled. Consequences to follow.
10. Before parting, we indicate that it is for the authorities
concerned and for the investigating authority to go into the
factual aspect, including the issue of whether the lands in
question were rightly given “No Dues Certificates” as also with

--- Page 36 ---
5
regard to the person, who was competent to do so.

         HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE SANDEEP MEHTA

Mr. Sankalp Mahindru, Adv.
Mr. Priyanshu, Adv.
           UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                        O R D E R
The petitioners seek anticipatory bail
in connection with FIR No.343 of 2023 lodged
at Chiraiya P.S., District East Champaran,
                 SLP (Crl.)  No(s).  185  04/2026                   1

--- Page 41 ---
Bihar,  for  the  offences  punishable  under
Section 304B read with Section 34 of the
Indian Penal Code, 1860 (For short, ‘IPC’). 
Brief facts essential for disposal of
the special leave petition are stated below.
The deceased Rupkali Devi was married
to Lakshmi Sahani, s/o Jaga Sahani in the year
2018.  From their wedlock, a son was born.  In
the year 2019, Lakshmi Sahani passed away
because of illness.  Following  the
death of Lakshmi Sahani, the families, after
mutual discussion, agreed that Rupkali Devi
should be married to Prince Sahani (petitioner
No.2), the younger brother of Lakshmi Sahani.
Accordingly, both were married in the year
2020 and a son named ‘Master V’ was born from
their wedlock.
It is alleged that Rupkali Devi was
continuously  harassed  and  humiliated  on
account  of  demand  of  dowry  and,  as  a
culmination to the said cruel conduct, she was
done to death in the matrimonial home on 16 th
                 SLP (Crl.)  No(s).  185  04/2026                   2

--- Page 42 ---
July, 2023. Shri Harendra Sahani, father of
the deceased Rupkali Devi, lodged an FIR at
P.S. Chiraiya, District East Champaran, on
16th July, 2023 itself, arraigning her father-
in-law,  mother-in-law,  brother-in-law
(petitioner  No.1),  brother-in-law’s  wife
(petitioner  No.3)  and  the  husband  Prince
Sahani (petitioner No.2) as accused in the
case.  The  dead  body  of  Rupkali  Devi  was
subjected  to  post-mortem,  and  a  pertinent
finding was recorded by the Medical Jurist in
the post-mortem report, which requires special
emphasis and is noted hereinbelow:-
“On External Examination  – Ligature mark
found in the upper part of the neck which is
wider in left side extending from left angle
of mandible to right up to posterior part of
the right pinna having size-7" × 1" which is
prominent  in  left  side  progressively
diffusing in right side. Abrasion on the both
side of the shoulder of size-2" × 2".
On Internal Examination – 
Head-Meninges and brain tissue congested
and intact. 
Neck  –  Subcutaneous  Haemorrhage  found
underlying the ligature mark mentioned above.
Trachea  congested.  Thyroid  xxx(illegible)
cricoid cartilage intact.
….
In my opinion, the cause of death is Asphyxia
due to hanging.
                 SLP (Crl.)  No(s).  185  04/2026                   3

--- Page 43 ---
Caused by – Ligature material.
Time elapsed since death within 18 hrs to 36
hrs from the time of P.M. Examination.”
Apparently, thus, the position of the
muscles underneath the ligature mark was found
with subcutaneous haemorrhage and the trachea
was congested.
As per medical jurisprudence by Modi, in
cases of death by asphyxia, where subcutaneous
haemorrhage is found in the muscles underlying
the  ligature  mark,  the  same  would  be
suggestive of manual strangulation rather than
hanging. In sheer ignorance of the symptoms
underneath  the  ligature  mark,  the  medical
jurists expressed an opinion that the cause of
death was asphyxia due to hanging.
Be that as it may, during the course of
investigation,  the  father-in-law  of  the
deceased, Jaga Sahani, and the mother-in-law
of the deceased, Shri Devi, were granted pre-
arrest bail by this Court  vide order dated
14th October, 2024 passed in Special Leave
Petition (Crl.) No.9716 of 2024.
                 SLP (Crl.)  No(s).  185  04/2026                   4

--- Page 44 ---
The police, after investigation, filed a
partial chargesheet only against the father-
in-law of the deceased, Jaga Sahani, and the
mother-in-law of the deceased, Shri Devi, for
the offences punishable under Sections 498A
and 306 read with Section 34 IPC.
The  learned  jurisdictional  Magistrate,
vide order dated 18 th November, 2025 proceeded
to take cognizance and directed summoning of
Devlal Sahani, Anandi Devi and Prince Sahani,
i.e., the petitioners herein.  The petitioners
herein, approached the High Court of Patna
apprehending their arrest in connection with
the aforesaid FIR.  Such application preferred
by the petitioners has been rejected by the
learned  Single  Judge  vide order  dated  6 th
August, 2026 whereupon the petitioners are
before  us  by  way  of  the  special  leave
petition.
Mr. Ashwarya Sinha, learned AAG has put
in appearance on behalf of the respondent-
State on advance notice.
Having  heard  and  considered  the
                 SLP (Crl.)  No(s).  185  04/2026                   5

--- Page 45 ---
submissions advanced by the learned counsel
for the parties and after going through the
material available on record, we are of the
opinion  that  the  case  of  Devlal  Sahani
(petitioner  No.1  –  brother-in-law  of  the
deceased) and Anandi Devi (petitioner No.3 –
sister-in-law  of  the  deceased)  is,  in  no
manner,  distinguishable  from  that  of  Jaga
Sahani  and  Shri  Devi  (father-in-law  and
mother-in-law of the deceased), who have been
extended the benefit of pre-arrest bail by
this Court. Hence, Devlal Sahani and Anandi
Devi (petitioner Nos.1 and 3) deserve the
indulgence of pre-arrest bail on parity.
However, Prince Sahani (petitioner No.2),
being the husband of the deceased Rupkali
Devi, was alleged to be the main offender in
the FIR. Otherwise also, the responsibility of
the husband to ensure the safety and security
of his wife is more onerous.
Hence,  we are  inclined to  extend the
indulgence of pre-arrest bail to Devlal Sahani
and Anandi Devi (petitioner Nos.1 and 3),
                 SLP (Crl.)  No(s).  185  04/2026                   6

--- Page 46 ---
whereas the prayer for pre-arrest bail made by
Prince  Sahani  (petitioner  No.2)  stands
rejected. 
Prince  Sahani  (petitioner  No.2)  shall
surrender before the trial Court within a
period of two weeks from today.
Before parting, we would like to observe
that the investigation seems to have been
misdirected in this case by the post-mortem
findings  referred  to  supra.  Therefore,  we
hereby  direct  that  the  Superintendent  of
AIIMS, Patna, shall constitute a Medical Board
which shall examine the post-mortem report and
the  antecedent  circumstances  and  give  its
opinion  regarding  the  cause  of  death  of
Rupkali Devi.
The opinion of the Medical Board shall be
forwarded to the trial Court, and a copy
thereof shall also be forwarded to the High
Court of Judicature at Patna for necessary
follow up action. In case the Medical Board is
of the opinion that the cause of death of
Rupkali  Devi  was  homicidal,  necessary
                 SLP (Crl.)  No(s).  185  04/2026                   7

--- Page 47 ---
amendment in the charges would follow.
We make it clear that the trial of the
case  shall  not  be  prejudiced  by  the
observations made hereinabove.
The special leave petition is disposed of
in these terms.
Pending  application(s),  if  any,  shall
also stand disposed of.

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
CIVIL APPELLATE JURISDICTION
CIVIL APPEAL NO.2637 OF 2012
   SMT. GOHAR SULTAN                        APPELLANT(S)
                                VERSUS
   SHEIKH ANIS AHMAD & ORS.                 RESPONDENT(S)
 
O R D E R
1. The present appeal has been filed assailing the
impugned judgment, dated 16.03.2011 in FAO No.267 of
2010, passed by the High Court of Delhi at New Delhi.
2. Brief  facts  relevant  for  the  purpose  of
determination of the present appeal are that the
respondent  No.1  in  the  present  appeal,  filed  a
petition under Section 278 of the Indian Succession
Act, 1925, seeking letters of administration on the
basis of will dated 20.11.1984, allegedly executed by
the deceased testatrix. The said proceedings were
contested by the appellant, relying upon another will
dated 05.06.1992, which was alleged to have been
executed by the deceased testatrix.
3. The  Trial  Court,  vide  impugned  order  dated
03.07.2010,  dismissed  the  petition  filed  by  the
respondent No.1, by accepting the will executed in
favour  of  the  appellant  as  genuine  and  valid.
1

--- Page 49 ---
Aggrieved by the same, the respondent No.1 approached
the High Court, wherein, the appeal was allowed and
the matter was remanded back to the Trial Court to
decide the issues afresh. Aggrieved by the same, the
appellant has preferred the present appeal. 
4. We have heard the learned Senior Counsel appearing
for the parties.
5. Though the learned Senior Counsel appearing for
the appellant raised four legal issues, we find that
the present suit has emanated against proceedings
seeking letters of administration under Section 278
of Indian Succession Act, 1925, which have been
converted into a testamentary original suit.
6. In our considered view, in the teeth of Sections
57 and 58 read with Section 213 of the Indian
Succession Act, 1925, at the relevant point of time,
the very petition instituted by the respondent No.1
itself is not maintainable.
7.  Our views are only limited for the purpose of the
question of maintainability of the petition.
8.  In such view of the matter, we are inclined to
set aside the impugned judgment dated 16.03.2011
passed by the High Court in FAO No.267/2010 and also
dismiss the Petition No.84/1994; Petition No.326/2006
(New), even though it enures to the benefit of the
appellant.
9.  We have been informed at the Bar, that another
2

--- Page 50 ---
suit seeking declaration of ownership and possession
filed by the respondent No.1 in OS No.405/2009 is
pending before the Additional District Judge, Tis
Hazari Courts, Delhi.
10. Since a suit has already been instituted by the
respondent  No.1  seeking  possession  of  the  land,
wherein, the evidence has already been led by the
parties, be that as it may, the concerned Trial Court
is directed to frame an additional issue  qua the
validity of the wills purported to be executed in
favour of both the sides. 
11. The concerned Trial Court shall afford adequate
opportunity to the parties to lead written evidence
in  support  of  their  relied-upon  wills  dated
20.11.1984 and 05.06.1992, respectively.
12. Taking into consideration the long pendency of
the dispute, a request is made to the concerned Trial
Court to expedite the hearing and decide the suit,
within a period of one year from the date of receipt
of a copy of this order.
13.  All issues including the legal issues sought to
be raised are left open to be decided by the Trial
Court.
14. Needless to say that the present order is passed
only with respect to the question of maintainability
of the petition under Section 278 of the Indian
Succession Act, 1925, which is the subject-matter of
3

--- Page 51 ---
the present appeal alone and, therefore, all other
legal issues pertaining to the dispute are left open.
15.  In  such  view  of  the  matter,  the  impugned
judgments passed by the High Court dated 16.03.2011,
as well as of the Trial Court dated 18.01.1995, are
set  aside,  holding  that  the  petition  filed  by
respondent No.1 is not maintainable.
16.  Accordingly, the appeal stands disposed of.
17.  Pending  application(s),  if  any,  shall  stand
disposed of. 
  ……………………………………………………………………J.
              [M.M. SUNDRESH]
       ………………………………………………………………………J.
         [PRASANNA B. VARALE]
 NEW DELHI;
      SEPTEMBER 16, 2026.
4

                             O R D E R
We have heard the learned counsel appearing for the parties.
Learned counsel appearing for the petitioner(s) shall cure the
defects in I.A. Diary No. 300878/2026, seeking permission to delete
the names of respondent Nos. 3 to 60 in SLP(C) No. 27933/2026 and
respondent Nos. 7 to 28 in SLP(C) No. 27934/2026 from the array of
parties, within a period of one week.
Insofar as the other unserved respondents in the connected
Special Leave Petitions are concerned, since the service has
already  been  effected  upon  the  respondents  in  the  connected
matters, who are represented through their respective learned
counsel, they shall be treated as served. 
With  respect  to  the  remaining  unserved  respondents,  the
petitioner(s) shall take steps afresh for effecting service upon
such respondents.
I.A.No.288019/2026  in  SLP(C)No.18859/2026  and  I.A.No.
290720/2026  in  SLP(C)No.2432-2436/2026  seeking  appropriate
directions regarding publication of notice is allowed.
The petitioner(s) shall give effect to the publication of
notice in one leading newspaper in the vernacular language and one
leading newspaper in the English language, having wide circulation
in the locality concerned. 
An affidavit indicating the proof of service of notice through

7
paper publication shall be filed by the petitioner(s), within a
period of four weeks thereafter.
All the intervention application(s) are allowed. 
Learned counsel appearing for the private respondents submit
that the particulars of the deceased respondents shall be furnished
to the learned counsel appearing for the petitioner(s) within a
period of two weeks from today.
In the meantime, pleadings be completed.
Tag these matters along with Special Leave Petition (Civil)
Diary No(s). 52934/2026.

            IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
   CRIMINAL APPELLATE JURISDICTION
         CRIMINAL APPEAL No.  4646/2026
[Arising out of SLP (Crl.) No.15621/2026]
PRABHULAL & ANR.                                 APPELLANTS
VERSUS
STATE OF RAJASTHAN                     RESPONDENT
        WITH
CRIMINAL APPEAL No.  4647/2026
[Arising out of SLP (Crl.) No.15592/2026]
HARISH & ORS.                                      APPELLANTS
VERSUS
STATE OF RAJASTHAN                   RESPONDENT
O R D E R
1. Leave granted.
2. The High Court of Judicature for Rajasthan, Bench at
Jodhpur  has  declined  the  prayers  of  the  appellants  –
1

--- Page 70 ---
Prabhulal, Sohanlal, Harish, Bherulal, Babulal, Shankar Lal,
Rakesh, Anil Kalasua, Narayan, Dinesh - for grant of bail.
Their applications1 stand dismissed by the common judgment
and order dated 23rd July, 2026.
3. These appeals take exception to such dismissal.
4. Appellants figure as accused in FIR No.0159 of 2026
dated 06th June, 2026 registered at Police Station Rishabhdev,
District Udaipur under Sections 299, 302 & 196(1)(a) of the
Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita, 2023 and Sections 3 & 5(1) of the
Rajasthan Prohibition of Unlawful Conversion of Religion Act,
2025. While the appellants, namely, Prabhulal and Sohanlal,
have been in judicial custody since 14 th June, 2026, the
appellants, namely, Harish, Bherulal, Babulal, Shankar Lal,
Rakesh, Anil Kalasua, Narayan and Dinesh, have been in
judicial custody since 6th June, 2026.
5.  We have heard learned counsel for the parties and
perused  the  counter-affidavit  of  the  respondent-State  of
Rajasthan.
6. It appears from the counter-affidavit  that chargesheet
1 S.B.  Criminal  Miscellaneous  Bail  Application  Nos.9344/2026  &
8501/2026
2

--- Page 71 ---
has been filed and the p rosecution proposes to record the
evidence of around 30 (thirty) witnesses to drive home the
charges against the appellants. However, the charges have
not yet been framed. Thus, it is quite unlikely that the trial
will conclude soon. We also find that 14th October, 2026 is the
next date fixed before the trial court for arguments on
charge.
7. It further appears from the counter affidavit that the
appellants have no criminal antecedents, and no co-accused
has been enlarged on bail. 
8. Learned counsel for the respondent-State of Rajasthan
does not dispute that the offence alleged to have been
committed by the appellants is triable by a magistrate.
9. Taking an overall view of the matter, we are of the
considered opinion that further detention of the appellants in
custody pending trial is not necessary; and, since the appeals
deserve acceptance, the appellants may be admitted to an
order for grant of bail.
10. Accordingly,  we  set  aside  the  common  impugned
judgment and order.
3

--- Page 72 ---
11. Appellants  shall  be  released  on  bail,  subject  to
furnishing of bail bonds to the satisfaction of the trial court
and subject to such other terms and conditions as may be
imposed by it.
12. Needless to observe, if released on bail, the appellants
shall not, directly or indirectly, by making inducement, threat
or promise, dissuade any person acquainted with the facts of
the case from disclosing such facts to the court.
13. In the event there is any breach of the terms and
conditions for grant of bail, the trial court shall be at liberty
to cancel the bail of the appellants.
14. It is also ordered that the appellants shall diligently
attend proceedings of the trial, unless exempted. If they
abstain from attending the proceedings without justifiable
cause, that could also be seen as breach of the conditions for
grant  of  bail  and  the  trial  court  will  be  free  to  pass
appropriate orders.
15. We clarify that the observations made in this order and
grant of bail will not be treated as findings on the merits of
the case.
4

--- Page 73 ---
16. The appeals are, accordingly, allowed on the aforesaid
terms.
17. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand disposed of.
............................................J.
(DIPANKAR DATTA)
............................................J.
(SHEEL NAGU)
New Delhi;
September 30, 2026.

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
INHERENT JURISDICTION
CONTEMPT PETITION (CIVIL) No.  336 OF 2026
IN
SPECIAL LEAVE PETITION (CIVIL) No. 15202 OF 2026
MD. TARIQUE      PETITIONER(S)
VERSUS
T.V. SOMANATHAN,
SECRETARY, MINISTRY OF FINANCE, AND ORS.    RESPONDENT(S)
ORDER
1. We have heard Mr. Shadan Farasat, learned Senior
counsel for the petitioner and Mr. Ravi Prakash, learned
Senior counsel for Respondent No(s).2 to 7.
2. On 31.08.2026, we had passed the following order:
“1. Issue notice to respondent Nos.2 to 7.
2. Mr. Ravi Prakash, learned counsel submits
that he appears for respondent Nos.2 to 7. When
it was brought to his notice that contempt is a
quasi criminal matter, he submits that he has
the requisite instruction to appear on behalf

joined services.
2

--- Page 90 ---

O R D E R
1. Leave granted.
2. The High Court of Madhya Pradesh at Indore, by the
impugned judgment and order dated 15th July, 2026, has dismissed
the appellant’s application for regular bail1.
3. Appellant was arrested on 8th September, 2025 in connection
with  FIR  Nos.  RC0082025S0010,  RC0082025S0011  and
RC0082025S0012 dated 9th May, 2025 registered at Police Station
CBI, ACB, District – Bhopal (M.P.) under Sections 420, 467, 468, 471
& 120-B of the Indian Penal Code, 1860, Section 66-D of the
Information Technology Act, 2000 and Section 7 of the Prevention of
Corruption Act, 1988.
1 MCRC 28180/2026, MCRC 27626/2026 and MCRC 27497/2026

--- Page 94 ---
2
4. We have heard learned counsel appearing for the parties and
perused the materials on record.
5. The police report (charge-sheet) has been filed on 16 th
September, 2025 and the charges have been framed on 9 th March,
2026. Trial has just commenced. Evidence of only one out of more
than a hundred witnesses the prosecution proposes to examine to
drive home the charges against the appellant has been recorded.
Trial is, thus, likely to take time to conclude. 
6. However,  having  regard  to  the  period  of  incarceration
suffered by the appellant coupled with the fact that the trial will be
a protracted exercise, we are inclined to accept the appeals.
7. Accordingly, we set aside the impugned judgment and order.
8. Appellant shall be released on bail, subject to furnishing of
bail bonds to the satisfaction of the trial court and subject to such
other terms and conditions as may be imposed by it.
9. Needless to observe, the appellant shall not, directly or
indirectly, by making inducement, threat or promise, dissuade any
person acquainted with the facts of the case from disclosing such
facts to the court.
10. In the event there is any breach of the terms and conditions
for grant of bail, the trial court shall be at liberty to cancel the bail
of the appellant.

--- Page 95 ---
3
11. It is also ordered that the appellant shall diligently attend
proceedings of the trial, unless exempted. If he abstains from
attending the proceedings without justifiable cause, that could also
be seen as breach of the conditions for grant of bail and the trial
court will be free to pass appropriate orders.
12. We clarify that the observations made in this order and grant
of bail will not be treated as findings on the merits of the case.
13.  The appeals are, accordingly, allowed on the aforesaid terms.
14.  Pending interlocutory application(s), if any, stand disposed
of.
15. Since offences under the Prevention of Money Laundering act,
2002 are alleged, the Enforcement Directorate will proceed with the
investigation according to law.

Leave granted.
We  have  heard  the  learned  Senior  Counsel
appearing for the parties.
In Criminal Appeals arising out of SLP(Crl.)
No.10957/2026 and SLP(Crl) No.10959/2026, the issue
raised by the learned Senior Counsel appearing for
the appellant pertains to the rigour of Section
21(4) of the Maharashtra Control of Organized Crime
Act, 1999 (for short ‘MCOCA’) and in the Criminal
Appeals arising out of SLP(Crl) No.10960/2026 and
SLP(Crl) No.11015/2026, the issue pertains to the
criminal antecedents of the respondent(s) not having
been considered by the High Court.

--- Page 99 ---
2
We have perused the impugned judgment(s) passed
by the High Court.
On perusing the impugned judgment, we find that
notwithstanding the fact that the prior criminal
antecedents of the respondent(s) were brought to the
notice of the High Court, they have not been duly
taken note of. We are of the further view that the
issue(s) pertaining to rigour under Section 21(4) of
the MCOCA have also not been duly considered.
In such view of the matter, we set aside the
impugned order(s) passed by the High Court and remit
the  matters  to  the  High  Court  for  a  fresh
consideration.
We request the High Court to consider the bail
applications filed by the respondent(s) on their own
merits afresh, in accordance with law and without
being  influenced  by  any  of  the  earlier  orders
passed.
Taking note of the facts and circumstances, we
request the High Court to pass appropriate orders,
within a period of four weeks from the date of
receipt of a copy of this order.
All the issues are left open to be raised by the
parties.
The appeals are disposed of, accordingly.

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA 
CRIMINAL APPELLATE JURISDICTION 
 
CRIMINAL APPEAL NO.                          OF 2026 
(Arising out of SLP (Crl) No.17917 of 2026) 
 
 
Surendra Jain @ Sattu                     ...Appellant(s) 
 
 
Versus 
 
State of Madhya Pradesh                          ...Respondent(s) 
 
with 
 
CRIMINAL APPEAL NO.                          OF 2026 
(Arising out of SLP (Crl) No.17944 of 2026) 
 
O R D E R 
 
Leave granted. 
2. Real Estate is a volatile business, and many a time, the 
discussions between real estate dealers  too become 
explosive. One such incident resulted in the 2 nd respondent 
being shot at , causing an injury on his leg, which led to a 
prosecution being launched on the charge of an attempt to 
murder. Two persons were arrayed as accused, one who 
exhorted to kill, and the other who shot. 
3. The appellant s along with one another  who were 
present on the occasion , were arrayed as witnesses ; no 

--- Page 104 ---
Page 2 of 5 
 
 
allegation having been raised, even by the 2nd respondent at 
the first instance  against them.  Later the 2 nd respondent 
turned around and accused the three bystanders of having 
been involved in a larger conspiracy. After many failed 
attempts, the 2 nd respondent was successful when the 3rd 
Additional Sessions Judge, Sagar  (MP), allowed the 
application filed by the prosecution under Section 319 of 
Code of Criminal Procedure, 1973 1, to summon the 
appellants. A challenge made to the order of the 3rd 
Additional Sessions Judge , Sagar (MP) , produced as 
Annexure P8 , resulted in the impugned order of the High 
Court; which too refused to entertain the challenge. 
4. We heard Sri Amit Anand Tiwari, learned Senior 
Counsel for the appellants and Sri B.P. Singh, learned D.A.G 
for the respondent State and Smt. Srishti Juneja, learned AoR 
for the 2nd respondent. 
5. The allegation in the First Information Statement (FIS)  
dated 22.02.2023  and the Section 161 statement of 
23.02.2023, were in tandem. The 2nd respondent alleged that 
he was engaged in talks with the two accused, Surendra Jain, 
Aabid Khan and Padam Jain , in the latter’s office premises. 
The 2nd respondent was looking for a deal to sell four duplex 
 
1 In short ‘CrPC’ 

--- Page 105 ---
Page 3 of 5 
 
 
units and five plots to the others , who were also property 
dealers. The price offered did not find favour with the 2 nd 
respondent and he walked out, when Aabid Khan interceded, 
consoled the 2 nd respondent and brought him back. While 
discussions were going on and the 2 nd respondent did not 
agree to the further offers made, one of the accused Nilesh 
Chaudhary is said to have exhorted when Deepak Shukla, the 
other accused, pulled out a pistol and shot the appellant  
twice, causing injury on his leg. Both the accused fled and 
Padam Jain and Aabid Khan took the victim to the hospital.  
6. A complaint was filed under Section 200 CrPC and a 
statement of the 2 nd respondent was taken under oath on 
15.10.2024, wherein he made very serious embellishments . 
He alleged that when he walked out, being not satisfied with 
the price offered, the others forcibly brought him inside and 
started abusing him. All of them together restrained him and 
Deepak Shukla is said to have fired at him. We cannot but 
notice that there are embellishments made , which the 2 nd 
respondent did not speak of  either in the FIS recorded on 
22.02.2023 or the Section 161 statement recorded on 
23.02.2023. 
7. Be that as it may, the application filed under Section 
200 CrPC stood rejected by the Judicial Magistrate First 

--- Page 106 ---
Page 4 of 5 
 
 
Class, Sagar (MP) on 14. 11.2024, finding no circumstance 
existing to take action against Aabid Khan and the appellants 
herein.  
8. Undeterred, the appellants filed a revision before the 
Additional Sessions Court and the prosecution filed an 
application, produced as Annexure P6 , on 23.06.2025 
seeking that the appellants and Aabid Khan be summoned 
under Section 319 CrPC. Immediately thereafter, the 9 th 
Additional Sessions Judge, Sagar (MP) rejected the revision 
filed against the learned Magistrate’s order by Annexure P7. 
It is in this background that Annexure P8 order of the 3 rd 
Additional Sessions Judge, Sagar, has to be looked at , 
summoning the witnesses as accused. 
9. As we noticed, serious embellishments were made 
insofar as the very incident that occurred. At the first 
instance, the appellants were not at all accused of any overt 
act nor was there a case of conspiracy ; that the persons who 
gathered in Padam Jain’s office for discussions on real estate 
had conspired together to make an attempt on the life of the 
2nd respondent. The gross embellishments made later to the 
first statement, puts paid the allegation of conspiracy, and the 
attempt to rope in the other persons present in the meeting  

--- Page 107 ---
Page 5 of 5 
 
 
on such accusation  falls flat. There was not even a common 
intention alleged on the appellants or Aabid Khan. 
10. We find absolutely no reason to sustain the order of 
the 3rd Additional Sessions Judge, Sagar (MP), upheld by the 
High Court in the impugned order.  We set aside the orders 
of the 3 rd Additional Sessions Judge, Sagar  (MP), dated 
26.08.2025 and the impugned order of the High Court, which 
stand quashed. 
11. There shall be no proceedings taken against the 
appellants in the case registered as Sessions Trial No.227 of 
2023, pending before the 3 rd Additional Sessions Judge, 
Sagar (MP). 
12. The appeals stand allowed. 
13. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand rejected.

....……….……………………. J.

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
CRIMINAL APPELLATE JURISDICTION
CRIMINAL APPEAL NO.              OF 2026
(@ SPECIAL LEAVE PETITION (CRIMINAL) NO. 11951/2026)
RISHAB KESHARI @ RISAB KESHARI      APPELLANT(S)
VERSUS
THE STATE OF WEST BENGAL & ANR.       RESPONDENT(S)
ORDER
1. Leave granted.
2. We have heard learned counsel for the appellant and
learned counsel for the respondent-State of West Bengal.
3. This  appeal  takes  exception  to  the  order  dated
18.06.2026 passed by the High Court at Calcutta (briefly
the ‘High Court’ hereinafter), rejecting the prayer for
anticipatory bail of the appellant.
4. It is stated that appellant has been arrayed as an
accused in FIR No.156/2025 registered at Police Station-
Naihati, District- Barrackpore, West Bengal under Section
107 of the Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita, 2023 (for short,
‘BNS’).
5. Allegation against the appellant is that he had
abetted in the commission of suicide by the victim. As per
the prosecution case, appellant and the victim were in a
relationship but when he refused to carry forward the
1

--- Page 117 ---
relationship, she committed suicide on 15.06.2025.
6. This Court, by order dated 07.07.2026, had issued
notice and granted interim protection to the appellant
subject  to  the  appellant  co-operating  with  the
investigation and submitting an undertaking before the
investigating  agency  that  he  would  not  threaten  the
witnesses or tamper with the evidence.
7. Learned  counsel  for  the  appellant  submits  that
pursuant to the aforesaid order of this Court, appellant
has  joined  investigation  and  has  submitted  the
undertaking.
8. Respondent-State of West Bengal has filed counter
affidavit. In addition to stating that the victim was a
minor, her date of birth as per the school certificate
being 08.08.2007, it is fairly stated that appellant has
joined the investigation and is co-operating with the
same.
9. Considering the above, we are of the view that the
interim protection granted by this Court vide the order
dated 07.07.2026 should be made absolute.
10. That being the position, we set aside the impugned
order dated 18.06.2026 passed by the High Court and make
our order dated 07.07.2026 absolute. However, we direct
that the appellant shall continue to co-operate with the
investigation of the case and shall not threaten the
witnesses or tamper with the evidence.
11. Consequently, the Criminal Appeal is allowed.
2

--- Page 118 ---
12. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand disposed
of.
…………………………………………………...J.
      
   [UJJAL BHUYAN]
…………………………………………………...J.

01st OCTOBER, 2026.

          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R

--- Page 125 ---
2
1. The applicant/appellant before this Court has filed the
present  Miscellaneous  Application  for  issuance  of
necessary direction arising out of Criminal Appeal No.
2276/2022.
2.  The facts of the case reveal that this Court in
Criminal Appeal No.2276/2022, “Vikas Chaudhary versus The
State of Delhi” with Criminal Appeal No.2277 of 2022 has
passed a judgment which was arising out of conviction in
which the appellant was imposed a fixed terms sentence of
30 years without remission. The sentence was affirmed by
the Delhi High Court and this Court while deciding the
matter(s) in paragraph Nos. 28 and 29 has held as under:-
“28. Both appellants in the present case, share
some commonalities: they were of young age at the
time of offence, hail from educated backgrounds,
and they continue to enjoy the love and affection
of their families, each of which have a good
standing and strong ties within the communities
they live in. While the material relating to their
lives and social conditions pre-conviction do not
offer an explanation as to the cause for commission
of offence, it can certainly be said that the
material available regarding their conduct post-
conviction, remains encouraging. They have applied
themselves during the time of incarceration and
used their time to contribute meaningfully – for
which they have each received commendations. Their
psychological  and  psychiatric  evaluations  were
concluded to be normal, without cause for concern.
A strong case is made out in support of the
appellants’  probability  of  reform  (as  already
evidenced by their jail conduct), and reintegration
into society. The state, too, has not indicated any
material to the contrary, regarding this aspect.
29. In view of the totality of the facts and

--- Page 126 ---
3
circumstances, and for the above reasons, this
court  is  of  the  opinion  that  it  would  be
appropriate to modify the sentence awarded to both
appellants to a minimum term of 20 years actual
imprisonment. The appeals are partly allowed in the
above terms.”
3.  The aforesaid paragraphs reveal that the sentence of
30 years rigorous imprisonment was modified to a minimum 20
years  actual  imprisonment.  The  applicant/appellant  has
undergone 20 years and 7 months of actual imprisonment.
4.  Mr. Davinder Pal Singh, learned Additional Solicitor
General for the Respondent-State prays for and is granted
four  weeks’  time  to  file  reply  in  the  matter.  Mrs.
Meenakshi  Arora,  learned  senior  counsel  for  the
applicant/appellant has vehemently argued before this Court
that the applicant/appellant has already completed minimum
term of 20 years of actual imprisonment and bail be granted
to him.
5.  This Court after hearing the learned counsel for the
parties is of the opinion that as the applicant/appellant
has  completed  a  minimum  term  of  20  years  of  actual
imprisonment. He is entitled for grant of bail. It is
ordered accordingly.
6.  The applicant/appellant shall be released on bail,
as per the terms and conditions as may be imposed by the
trial court. As we have already granted bail to the
applicant/appellant, we are also granting liberty to the
applicant/appellant  to  file  application  through  proper
channel for pre-mature release within one week from today

--- Page 127 ---
4
and the State Government shall pass necessary order in
accordance with law in respect of applicant’s application
within three weeks thereafter.
7.  Let counter affidavit positively be filed within
four weeks.
8.  List the matter after four weeks.
  
(JAGDISH KUMAR)                                 (KOMAL)
ASTT. REGISTRAR-cum-PS                        COURT MASTER (NSH)

4. Taking note of the nature of the allegations

grant anticipatory bail to the appellant.

bail, subject to the terms and conditions that

continue  to  cooperate  with  the  pending

                       RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS
Petition for Special Leave to Appeal (C)  No.34034/2026
[Arising out of impugned final judgment and order dated
20-08-2026 in WPMB No. 144/2026 passed by the High Court of
Uttarakhand at Nainital]
KOTAK MAHINDRA BANK LIMITED                      Petitioner(s)
                                VERSUS
CHAIRPERSON DEBTS RECOVERY APPELLATE 
TRIBUNAL & ORS.    Respondent(s)
IA No. 294463/2026 - EXEMPTION FROM FILING C/C OF THE IMPUGNED
JUDGMENT
 
Date : 29-09-2026 This matter was called on for hearing today.
CORAM :  HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE J.B. PARDIWALA
         HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE K. VINOD CHANDRAN
For Petitioner(s) :Mr. Amar Dave, Sr. Adv.
                   Mr. Pranav Sarthi- 3598, AOR
                   Mr. Vishal Bhardwaj, Adv.
                   Mr. Ayush Raj, Adv.
                   Mr. Udit Bajpai, Adv.
                                   
For Respondent(s) :Mr. Shri Venkatesh, Adv.
                   Mr. Shryeshth Ramesh Sharma, Adv.
                   Mr. Soham Goswami, Adv.
1

--- Page 141 ---
2
SLP (C)  No.34034/2026
                   Mr. Kunal Veer Chopra, Adv.
                   Mr. Sanyam Agarwal, Adv.
                   Mr. Farrukh Rasheed- 1993, AOR
                                 
Upon hearing the counsel, the Court made the following
O R D E R
The petitioner-Bank is aggrieved by the order passed
by the High Court in a challenge to the order of the
Debts Recovery Appellate Tribunal (DRAT).
2. The impugned order directed the petitioner-Bank to
file a supplementary affidavit in three weeks, indicating
how the original loan amount of Rs.32 lakhs has swelled
to Rs.19 crores and odd, during the pendency of the suit.
It was also directed that the Bank would comply with the
direction relating to deposit of title deeds with the
Debts Recovery Tribunal (DRT) as it is not disputed that
the entire amount, as per the decree of the DRT and
upheld by the DRAT, has already been deposited by the
borrowers.
3. Learned Senior Counsel for the petitioner argued that
the order prejudices the petitioner-Bank insofar as the
DRAT has directed return of the title documents, which
2

--- Page 142 ---
3
SLP (C)  No.34034/2026
are mortgaged to the petitioner-Bank. Insofar as the
interest computed, it is asserted that the loan was
pending for about 30 years, and even if simple interest
is applied, it would run to Crores. The interest has to
be applied as per the agreement, which also has penalty
clauses, is the submission of the learned Senior Counsel
for the petitioner-Bank.
4. Learned  Counsel  for  the  respondent  appears  and
submits that the entire amount, as decreed by the DRT,
has been paid by the respondent Nos.3-6/borrowers and
hence, the direction to return the title deeds.
5. True, the DRT had adjudicated the claim and arrived
at a figure which was challenged by the petitioner-Bank
before the DRAT. The DRAT having affirmed the order of
the DRT, the petitioner is before the High Court.
6. We are of the opinion that it would be premature to
direct the release of the title deeds to the party
respondents/borrowers. However, we do not think it proper
to interfere with the direction of the High Court to
furnish a Statement of Account through a supplementary
affidavit  justifying  the  outstanding  amount  and  the
3

--- Page 143 ---
4
SLP (C)  No.34034/2026
direction to deposit the title deeds before the DRT. We
make it clear that on deposit of such title deeds, it
shall  not  be  returned  to  the  respondent  Nos.3-
6/borrowers. The DRT shall await further order of the
High Court and, if it is prejudicial to the petitioner-
Bank,  shall  forebear  during  the  period  when  the
petitioner-Bank is entitled to challenge the said order. 
7. With  the  above  direction  and  modification,  the
Special Leave Petition stands disposed of without any
observation on the merits of the matter.
8. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand rejected.
(CHANDRESH)                                  (POOJA SHARMA)
ASTT. REGISTRAR-cum-PS                      COURT MASTER (NSH)
4

--- Page 144 ---

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
CIVIL ORIGINAL JURISDICTION
TRANSFER PETITION (C) NO.1615/2026
ROOHEE KALIM GANI                                             PETITIONER
                                VERSUS
AZHAR HUSSAIN                                      RESPONDENT
O R D E R
1. Read the office report dated 29th September, 2026.
2. Despite service of notice of this transfer petition under Section 25 of
the Code of Civil Procedure, 1908 presented by Roohee Kalim Gani (hereafter
the  petitioner)  on  Azhar  Hussain  (hereafter  the  respondent),  he  has  not
entered appearance.
3. The  marriage  between  the  petitioner  and  the  respondent  was
solemnized on 29 th April, 2024. No child is born to the petitioner and the
respondent from the wedlock. However, the parties have since drifted apart
owing to differences arising by and between them.
4. The respondent has instituted proceedings under the Muslim Marriage
Act, 1939 and Muslim Marriage and Divorce Registration Act, 1975, bearing
O. S. No.55 of 2026 titled “Azhar Hussain Shaik vs. Roohee Kalim Gani” which
is pending before the court of the Principal Junior Civil Judge cum Judicial
Magistrate First Class at Ranjanna, Sircilla, T elangana.
5. On the multiple grounds urged in the petition, the petitioner has sought
for transfer of the aforesaid proceedings from the court of Principal Junior
Civil Judge cum Judicial Magistrate First Class at Ranjanna, Sircilla, T elangana
1

--- Page 149 ---
to a competent court at Bandra, Mumbai, Maharashtra.
6. Since the respondent has not controverted the statements/averments
made in the petition, we are of the view that he has no serious objection to
grant of the prayer for transfer.
7. We are also otherwise satisfied, having regard to the inconvenience the
petitioner is likely to face if the proceedings were continued in the court at
Ranjanna, Sircilla, T elangana (at a distance of around 840 kms. from her
present place of residence), that interest of justice demands grant of the
prayer for transfer.
8. Hence,  the  transfer  petition  is  allowed.  O.  S.  No.55  of  2026  titled
“Azhar Hussain Shaik vs. Roohee Kalim Gani” pending before the court of the
Principal Junior Civil Judge cum Judicial Magistrate First Class at Ranjanna,
Sircilla, T elangana is transferred to the competent court at Bandra, Mumbai,
Maharashtra. Records of O. S. No.55 of 2026 be transferred to the transferee
court immediately .
9. T o avoid delay, the parties are directed to appear before the transferee
court on 02 nd November, 2026. If any or both the parties fail to appear on
that day, the presiding officer of the transferee court will fix another date of
appearance. Thereafter, he shall be free to proceed in accordance with law.
10. The  presiding  officer  shall  endeavour  to  bring  about  a  settlement
between  the  parties  through  the  process  of  mediation.  If  the  process  is
unsuccessful,  nothing  shall  preclude  the  presiding  officer  to  take  the
proceedings to its logical conclusion on its merits and in accordance with law.
11. If the respondent is unable to attend the proceedings in person on any
occasion,  he  will  be  at  liberty  to  seek  permission  to  attend  through  the
2

--- Page 150 ---
virtual mode and if such a permission is sought, the presiding officer of the
court may proceed to consider it reasonably .
12. Pending application(s), if any, shall also stand disposed of.
………..……………………...J.
[DIPANKAR DATTA]  
 ..……………………………..J.
[SHEEL NAGU]  
New Delhi;
September 30, 2026.

CRIMINAL APPEAL NO. 2283/2014
PENDIL RAVINDER                                   Appellant(s)
                                VERSUS
THE STATE OF ANDHRA PRADESH                      Respondent(s)
O R D E R
1. The present Criminal Appeal has been filed against the
judgment of conviction dated 01.05.2007 passed by the First
Additional Session Court, Karimnagar convicting the appellant
for the offences punishable under sections 302 and 411 of
Indian Penal Code. The judgment of conviction dated 01.05.2007
has been affirmed in Criminal Appeal No. 924/2007 by the High
Court of Andhra Pradesh at Hyderabad.
2. The  conviction  of  the  appellant  is  based  on
circumstantial evidence. As per the prosecution case one Mada
Devender Reddy who was known to the accused was last seen with
the accused on 13.09.2005 along with the cash of Rs. 50,000/-
(Rs. Fifty thousand only). They went to Bhulaxmi Lodge on
14.09.2005 and stayed in a room. The Management of the lodge
noticed the death of the deceased in the room and the matter
was investigated.
3. There is an evidence of last seen together and the

--- Page 153 ---
2
witnesses supported the same. The appellant has also been
identified by the witnesses because as many as 19 witnesses
were examined and there is a recovery of money which as per
the prosecution case, belong to the deceased. As the chain of
events  is  complete,  the  Trial  Court  has  convicted  the
appellant  and  thereafter  the  High  Court  affirmed  this
conviction.
4. In the present case, life imprisonment was awarded to the
appellant for the offences punishable under Section 302 IPC
and  03  years  rigorous  imprisonment  was  awarded  for  the
offences punishable under Section 411 of IPC.
5. The order passed by the learned Sessions Judge reveals
that the appellant was arrested on 10.10.2005 at 06:00 a.m.
and he is in custody since then, meaning thereby he is behind
bars for more than 20 years.
6. We do not find any reason to interfere with the judgment
of conviction passed by the Trial Court and affirmed by the
High Court. However, as the appellant has undergone the
sentence of more than 20 years, we are of the opinion that the
life imprisonment awarded to the appellant deserves to be
reduced to the period of sentence already undergone. Ordered
accordingly.
7. The appeal is partly allowed only to the extent of period
of the imprisonment.
8. The appellant is directed to be released forthwith if not

--- Page 154 ---
3
required in any other case.
9. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand disposed of.
…………………………………………………………………. J.
 (SATISH CHANDRA SHARMA)
……….…………………………………………………………………. J.
 (NONGMEIKAPAM KOTISWAR SINGH)
New Delhi,
30th September, 2026

--- Page 155 ---

          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following

1. The matter was taken up today for hearing in the Committee
Room. Petitioner – Ms. Nandita Chaudhary is personally present

--- Page 173 ---
2
before us. Respondent – Mr. Tarun Trikha, is also present before
us. We had quite an exhaustive interaction with the parties.  We
also heard the learned counsel appearing for the parties. 
2. We have reached to the conclusion after talking to the parties
that it is difficult to save the marriage. There is no doubt that
the case is one of an irretrievable breakdown of marriage. We made
the parties conscious of the fact that they have a son Reyaan.
Reyaan  is  8  years  of  age  as  on  date.  Whatever  may  be  the
differences between the husband and wife, at least both should
stand together when it comes to the welfare and interest of Reyaan.
There are proposals and counter-proposals from both the sides for
the purpose of putting an end to all the disputes and also the
marriage. 
3. We do not want to observe anything further in details today,
however, we have put forward two proposals before the Respondent,
i.e., (i) of making a lump sum payment and get a good accommodation
on rental basis in Gurugram and pay the rent as also monthly
expenses which earlier the respondent used to pay  Rs.6,00,000/-
(Rupees Six Lakh only) per month towards the maintenance of his
wife and son Reyaan.  Insofar as school fees and other expenses
towards Reyaan is concerned, it shall be borne by the father. 
4. The Respondent has agreed to shoulder all the responsibility
insofar as school fees, tuition fees and other expenses that the
mother may have to incur for Reyaan etc. We are further informed
that there are some outstanding bills insofar as the present
accommodation is concerned. The petitioner is being harassed in
this regard by the Society etc. We want the respondent to take care
of this problem and see to it that the outstanding bills etc. are
cleared within a period of one week from today.  There is one
insurance policy whose premium is to be paid by 5th October, 2026.
The respondent shall see to it that this amount is paid on or
before 5th October, 2026, otherwise the policy would lapse.  We say
so because it will be in the interest of his son Reyaan. The
respondent wants some time to revert on the two proposals we have
noted above. However, till the next date of hearing, the respondent

--- Page 174 ---
3
shall ensure that the mother and son are in no manner disturbed
from the accommodation provided at present.
5. List the matter on 25-11-2026 in the Committee Room.
6. The matter shall be treated as part-heard.

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA 
CIVIL APPELLATE JURISDICTION 
CIVIL APPEAL NO. 6999 OF 2019
ASCEND ENGINEERING CONTRACTORS       ...APPELLANT
VERSUS
M/S. ELECON EPC PROJECTS LIMITED ...RESPONDENT
O R D E R
1. Heard Ms. Ramsha Hashmi, learned counsel appearing
for the appellant and Ms. Madhvi G. Diwan, learned senior
counsel appearing for the respondent.
2. Ms. Ramsha Hashmi, learned counsel, has taken us
through the events that have transpired after completion of
work and the correspondence that was exchanged with the
respondent. She has also taken us through the reasoning
adopted by the High Court while rejecting the application
under Section 11 of the Arbitration and Conciliation Act,
1996  (for  short,  the  ‘Act’).  In  furtherance  of  her
submission, Ms. Ramsha Hashmi has also relied on decisions
of this Court in “ Geo Miller & Company Pvt. Ltd. v.
Chairman, Rajasthan Vidyut Utpadan Nigam Limited” 1 and
“Indian  Oil  Corporation  Limited.  v.  SPS  Engineering
Limited”2. 
1  (2020) 14 SCC 643
2    (2011) 3 SCC 507
1

--- Page 197 ---
3. Having considered the matter in detail, we are of the
opinion that the conclusions of the High Court that the
petition  under  Section  11  of  the  Act  is  barred  by
limitation is correct in law or fact.
4. Accordingly, the Civil Appeal stands dismissed. 
5. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand disposed
of.

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA 
CIVIL APPELLATE JURISDICTION 
CIVIL APPEAL NO. 11196 OF 2017
SHARADA ALIAS RADHABAI MANGUESH KENKRE 
WIDOW OF MANGUESH KENKRE & ORS. ...APPELLANTS
VERSUS
GURUDAS YESHWANT PEDNEKAR (SINCE DECEASED) 
REPRESENTED BY HIS LEGAL HEIRS     ...RESPONDENTS
O R D E R
1. The present appeal arises out of judgment and order
passed by the High Court of Bombay at Goa in Second Appeal
No. 44 of 2006 dated 31.01.2014. 
2. Heard Mr. Ishaan George, learned counsel appearing
for the appellants and Mr. Kailash Vasdev, learned senior
counsel appearing for the respondents.
3. Mr. Kailash Vasdev, learned senior counsel, submitted
that while the present suit is only for injunction, the
earlier suit filed by the respondents-plaintiffs for eviction
has  been  decreed  and  those  proceedings  have  attained
finality. He would further submit that the said decree has
been executed and the respondents-plaintiffs have taken
possession of the property.
1

--- Page 200 ---
4. Mr. Kailash Vasdev, learned senior counsel submits
that no further orders need to be passed in the present
civil appeal arising out of the suit for injunction between
the same parties and as regards the same property. 
5. Mr. Ishaan George, learned counsel submits that he
has no instructions about the handing over of possession.
We give him the liberty to file an appropriate application,
in the event, he is informed with necessary documents that
possession has not been taken over. 
6. Having considered the matter in detail, we are of the
opinion that the High Court has not committed any error on
fact or law in allowing the Second Appeal no. 44 of 2006 by
the order impugned before us.
7. In  view  of  the  above,  the  Civil  Appeal  stands
dismissed. 
8. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand disposed
of.

ITEM NO.8               COURT NO.6               SECTION II
               S U P R E M E  C O U R T  O F  I N D I A
                       RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS
SPECIAL LEAVE PETITION (CRIMINAL) Diary No(s). 58283/2026
[Arising out of impugned final judgment and order dated 19-05-2026
in CRLMBA No.43157/2023 passed by the High Court of Judicature at
Allahabad]
PRAVEEN                                            Petitioner
                                VERSUS
STATE OF UTTAR PRADESH                             Respondent
I.A. No.295755/2026-CONDONATION OF DELAY IN FILING
I.A. No.295754/2026-EXEMPTION FROM FILING C/C OF THE IMPUGNED 
JUDGMENT
I.A. No.295757/2026-EXEMPTION FROM FILING O.T.
 
Date : 01-10-2026 This matter was called on for hearing today.
CORAM :  HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE DIPANKAR DATTA
         HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE SHEEL NAGU
For Petitioner(s) :Ms. Prakriti Rastogi, Adv.
                   Mr. Azad Bansala, Adv.
                   Mr. Rishi Kapoor, AOR
                   Mr. Nadeem Arman, Adv.
                   Mr. Parth Kapoor, Adv.
                   Mr. Atharve Tiwari, Adv.                   
                   
For Respondent(s) : 
          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. Delay condoned.
2. Having regard to the order we propose to pass, the respondent-State need
not be served.  
3. The special leave petition is directed against an order dated 19 th May,
2026 whereby, in quick succession and for the third time, the High Court has
granted interim bail to the petitioner for a period of five months. The period of
1

--- Page 206 ---
bail is due to expire in the middle of this month.
4. The grievance of the petitioner is that his bail application has remained
pending since 2023 and no final decision is in sight. He is charged with murder.
5. There could be genuine reasons which have disabled the High Court from
disposing of the bail application finally. However, it goes without saying that
granting interim bail for five months successively for short periods without
disposal of the application bail may not be in the best interest of the petitioner
who has been languishing in jail since 16 th May, 2017. He is justified in his
contention that the bail application ought to be decided early, one way or the
other. 
6. In view thereof, we dispose of the special leave petition directing as
follows: -
(i) The interim bail granted to the petitioner which is due to expire in
the middle of October, 2026, shall stand extended till 31 st December,
2026, for the present;
(ii) The High Court may assign priority to the application for bail and
endeavor to decide the same finally by the end of this year;
(iii) In the unlikely event of the High Court being disabled to decide
the application finally in terms of direction (ii) above, it may consider
the desirability of securing the liberty of the petitioner for such further
time till the application is disposed of; and
(iv) The High Court may pass necessary direction(s) to facilitate
completion of the trial without any delay.
7. Pending application(s), if any, shall also stand disposed of.
8. Needless to observe, the application for bail shall be decided on its own
merits and this order may not be construed as an expression of opinion on
2

--- Page 207 ---
the merits of the petitioner’s claim that he is innocent and has been
falsely implicated. 
  (RASHMI DHYANI PANT)                       (SUDHIR KUMAR SHARMA)
 ASST. REGISTRAR-CUM-PS                        COURT MASTER (NSH)
 
3

--- Page 208 ---
1

          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. Mr. Siddharth Dharmadhikari, learned Standing Counsel for the State of
Maharashtra, alleges suppression of material facts by the petitioners and
contends that they are disentitled to any relief. Producing a copy of the order
dated 3 rd August, 2026 passed by the High Court, he submits that the
petitioners obtained an interim order without disclosing its contents; had such
contents been disclosed, this Court may not have entertained the special leave
petition.
2. Learned counsel appearing for the petitioners submits that in the rush to
file the special leave petition seeking protective relief urgently, due to
inadvertence, the order dated 3 rd August, 2026 could not be pleaded or
annexed.
3. Be that as it may, we find that the interim application filed by the
petitioners together with the contempt petitions filed by the petitioners and the
developer, represented by Mr. Gaurav Aggrawal, learned senior counsel, are
due to be listed before the Division Bench of the High Court on 6 th October,
2026.  Since only three days would intervene between today and the next date
of hearing, this Court enquired of Mr. Aggrawal for the developer as to whether
status  quo with regard to the disputed property could be maintained till 6 th
October, 2026. Reluctantly, he has agreed.
4. We leave it open to the Division Bench of the High Court to make such
appropriate order on the interim applications / contempt petitions as it deems
fit and proper on facts and in the circumstances.
5. Needless to observe, nothing contained herein shall be construed as
expression of opinion on the merits of the rival claims by this Court.

--- Page 210 ---
3
6. The special leave petition is, accordingly, disposed of on the above terms
cautioning the petitioners to be careful in future.
7. Pending interlocutory application(s), if any, shall also stand disposed of.

For Respondent(s) : 
          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. The present petition has been filed for cancellation of bail
granted by the High Court of Judicature at Patna in Criminal
Miscellaneous No.24056 of 2026, on the ground that the High Court
while granting bail has observed that dispute primarily appears to
be of civil nature.
2.  Learned senior counsel appearing on behalf of the petitioner
has argued before this Court that the observation made by the High
Court with regard to the nature of offence will come in the way of
investigation as well as before the Courts below.
PETITION(S) FOR SPECIAL LEAVE TO APPEAL (CRL.)  NO(S).  18123/2026

--- Page 270 ---
2
3. After hearing learned Senior Counsel for the petitioner and
perusing the impugned order, this Court is of the opinion that the
observations made by the High Court with regard to the nature of
offence, while granting bail to the accused, should not come in the
way of the rights and contentions of respective parties before the
Courts below.  Ordered accordingly. 
4. With the above observations, the special leave petition is
dismissed.
5. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand disposed of. 
of.

ITEM NO.12               COURT NO.15               SECTION II-A
               S U P R E M E  C O U R T  O F  I N D I A
                       RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS
PETITION(S) FOR SPECIAL LEAVE TO APPEAL (CRL.)  NO(S).  16643/2026
[Arising out of impugned final judgment and order dated 27-03-2026
in BA No. 1897/2025 passed by the High Court of Judicature at
Bombay at Aurangabad]
SATYENDRA RADHESHYAM MISHRA                        PETITIONER(S)
                                VERSUS
THE STATE OF MAHARASHTRA                           RESPONDENT(S)
 
Date : 01-10-2026 This petition was called on for hearing today.
CORAM :  HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE SATISH CHANDRA SHARMA
         HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE NONGMEIKAPAM KOTISWAR SINGH
For Petitioner(s) :Mr. Amit- 3306, AOR
                   Mr. Amit Chaubey, Adv.
                                      
For Respondent(s) :Mr. Raman Yadav, Adv.
                   Mr. Siddharth Dharmadhikari, Adv.
                   Mr. Aaditya Aniruddha Pande- 3259, AOR
                   Mr. Shrirang B. Varma, Adv.
                   
          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1.  The facts of the case reveal that the petitioner is
in jail since 15.04.2022 in connection with FIR No.39/2022,
dated 10.04.2022 registered at Police Station Malakoli,
District Nanded, Maharashtra for the offences punishable
under Sections 420, 468, 471 read with Section 34 of the
Indian Penal Code, 1860. The petitioner was granted bail by
the High Court of Judicature at Bombay Bench at Aurangabad
(for short ‘the High Court’) in Bail Application No.43 of
2023 vide order dated 23.02.2023 and he was directed to

--- Page 324 ---
deposit a sum of Rs.20,00,000/- (Twenty Lakh) before the
trial Court. The petitioner could not arrange the aforesaid
amount and, therefore, he was not released on bail. The
petitioner thereafter, as his mother was unwell, preferred
a bail application again for grant of temporary bail for 60
days vide Bail Application No.1897 of 2025 and the High
Court again dismissed the same vide order dated 27.03.2026,
by observing that as the petitioner has not deposited a sum
of Rs.20,00,000/- (Twenty Lakh), therefore, the question of
granting him temporary bail even for 60 days due to ill
health of his mother does not arise. The prayer has been
opposed by other side, however, the facts remains that the
petitioner is in custody for the last four and half years
and his mother is unwell. He is praying only for grant of
temporary bail for 60 days.
2.  After hearing learned counsel for the parties and
after going through the record, keeping in view the period
of incarceration of the petitioner and the fact that the
trial will take long time, this Court is of the opinion
that the petitioner is entitled for grant of regular bail.
Accordingly, the prayer for bail is allowed. The petitioner
is released on bail, subject to terms and conditions as may
be imposed by trial court.
3.  The Special Leave Petition is disposed of.
4.  Pending application(s), if any, shall stand disposed
of.     
(JAGDISH KUMAR)                                 (KOMAL)
ASTT. REGISTRAR-cum-PS                        COURT MASTER (NSH)

punishable under Sections 3(5), 316(5) and 318(4) of

vehemently  argued  before  this  Court  that  the
petitioners are employees of a cooperative society,
there is no likelihood of their absconding, and that

has  fairly  stated  before  this  Court  that  the
petitioners have joined the investigation.

the parties and after going through the record, is of
the opinion that the interim order dated 31.08.2026
deserves to be made absolute.

I. A No. 277532/2026
1. Vide this application, the applicant/petitioner (Sunil Kumar
Singh) seeks exemption from surrendering.
2. He was convicted under Section 364(A), 395 and 412 of IPC and
sentenced to imprisonment for life under Section 364(A) IPC, for 10
years under Section 395 IPC, and for 10 years under Section 412
IPC.

--- Page 338 ---
2
3. The appeal preferred by the applicant/petitioner (in Criminal
Appeal (DB) No. 403/2018) was dismissed by the High Court of
Judicature at Patna on 16.05.2025.
4. Ex-facie, a year and four months have gone by since dismissal
of his appeal but the applicant/ petitioner chose not to surrender.
5. It appears that even the authorities concerned have not taken
any measures to secure his custody either.
6.  Apparently,  the  application  lacks  bona  fides  and  is,
accordingly, dismissed.
7.  At  this  stage,  however,  learned  counsel  for  the
applicant/petitioner submits that the applicant be granted four
days to surrender. The prayer is granted. The applicant/petitioner
shall surrender on or before 6th October 2026. 
8. Learned counsel for the applicant shall furnish a specific
affidavit/certificate in this regard with the Registry/Office by 9th
October 2026. In the event of default, the office would Re-list the
matter for appropriate orders. 
(KANCHAN CHOUHAN)                            (PARVEEN KUMAR RAWAT)

        UPON hearing the counsel, the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. This special leave petition filed against the order dated
18.05.2026 passed in Writ Appeal No. 3517/2025, on the file of
the High Court of Madhya Pradesh, was entertained under the
impression that the High Court had reserved orders in the said
1

--- Page 382 ---
appeal on 26.05.2026. The interim order passed by this Court
on 27.05.2026 reflects that this Court was inclined to grant
interim relief based on the understanding that no measure of
protection  had  been  afforded  to  the  petitioners  pending
pronouncement of the judgment by the High Court. 
2. While so, we are now informed that the said writ appeal is
still pending and the High Court, thereafter, pronounced an
order on 25.05.2026, which was digitally signed on 27.05.2026,
rejecting the prayer of the petitioners for grant of stay. 
3. We are further informed that a separate special leave petition
has been filed against the said order, which is pending
consideration. 
4. In that view of the matter, this special leave petition no
longer survives for consideration. The same is, accordingly,
dismissed. 
Pending application(s), if any, shall stand disposed of.

approaching the Registry.

released to the applicant by demand draft within 14 days, after deducting the

ITEM NO.3               COURT NO.7               SECTION II-A
               S U P R E M E  C O U R T  O F  I N D I A
                       RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS
Petition(s) for Special Leave to Appeal (Crl.) No.14936/2026
[Arising out of impugned final judgment and order dated 09-02-2026
in BA No.11922/2025 passed by the High Court of Jharkhand at
Ranchi]
ASGAR ALI                                          Petitioner
                                VERSUS
THE STATE OF JHARKHAND                             Respondent
I.A. No.201949/2026-EXEMPTION FROM FILING C/C OF THE IMPUGNED 
JUDGMENT
I.A. No.201947/2026-EXEMPTION FROM FILING O.T.
 
Date : 28-09-2026 This matter was called on for hearing today.
CORAM :  HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE DIPANKAR DATTA
         HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE SHEEL NAGU
For Petitioner(s) :Mr. Awanish Sinha, AOR
                   Mr. Pankaj Kumar, Adv.
                   Mr. Sumit Kumar, Adv.
                   Mr. Sanjib Kumar Mohanthy, Adv.
                   Mr. Desh Ratan Nigam, Adv.                   
                   
For Respondent(s) :Ms. Tulika Mukherjee, AOR
                   Mr. Beenu Sharma, Adv.
                   Mr. Venkat Narayan, Adv.
                   Ms. Jyotsna Singh, Adv.
          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. Having regard to the fact that the charge-sheet has been filed on 12 th
January, 2026 against all three accused persons but, reportedly, charges could
not be framed because of repeated filing of bail applications by the co-accused
of the petitioner, we propose to dispose of the special leave petition without
interfering with the order impugned but encouraging the trial court to expedite
1

--- Page 462 ---
the process of framing of charges by passing an appropriate order in
accordance with law.
2. Ordered accordingly. 
3. Once the charges are framed, the trial court may expedite the trial too.
4. If there be any change of circumstances or no reasonable progress to
conclude the trial is achieved within one year next from date of framing of
charges despite full cooperation extended by the petitioner for concluding the
trial, he shall be at liberty to renew his prayer for bail before the jurisdictional

               S U P R E M E  C O U R T  O F  I N D I A
                       RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS

[Arising out of impugned final judgment and order dated 02-09-2026 
in CRM-M No. 42009/2026 passed by the High Court of Punjab & 
Haryana at Chandigarh]

                                VERSUS

1.  Mr. Girish Ananthamurthy, learned counsel submits that
on the day the father died i.e. 1981 with regard to the
father’s share, there will be a per stirpital notional
partition and to that extent partition should have been
ordered of the schedule B property also vis-a-vis the
father’s share. F ather having died in 1981,  under
Section 6 of the Hindu Succession Act, there would have
been  notional  partition  on  the  father’s  share  in
schedule  B  property,  is  the  submission  of  the
petitioner.

delay  as  well  as  on  the  Special  Leave  Petition,
returnable on 20.11.2026.
